# Computer Networking and Security (NET): past exam questions

Every question from the photographed sheets, books and PDFs in `ex-question/` that belongs to this subject. The same question asked in several exams, or printed in several sources, is shown once. For each question you get **where it was asked** (the exams, with names normalised in `../exam-tags.json`) and **where it was taken from** (each source document, linked to its transcription and image).

- **Distinct questions:** 1073 (from 1295 records: Written 908, MCQ 174, Study note 21, Worked example 3, Viva 189)
- **Exams it appears in:** 220
- **Corrections to printed answers:** 43
- **How counts work:** "Asked in (N)" counts different government/public-sector recruitment exams (BCS, NTRCA, BPSC, state banks, public bodies) and BUET M.Sc. admission tests. Private-bank exams and compiled viva experiences are listed but not counted. Coaching suggestion sheets and model tests never add to the count.

- **Printed answer:** 165.121→I0, 167.151→I1, 163.151→I2, 171.92→I3, 0.0.0.0→I4

  - 38th BCS Written (2018), Q4(b) — [BCS/25_ipv4-ipv6-addressing-classes.md](../BCS/25_ipv4-ipv6-addressing-classes.md) · [image](../BCS/25_ipv4-ipv6-addressing-classes.jpg)
  - Bit to Exabyte, Super ICT 150++ Questions Book (chapter-wise previous questions) — IP Address and Subnetting, Q47 — [bit to exabyte/05_bte-p005-ip-q46-49-raid-dbms-q01.md](../bit%20to%20exabyte/05_bte-p005-ip-q46-49-raid-dbms-q01.md) · [image](../bit%20to%20exabyte/05_bte-p005-ip-q46-49-raid-dbms-q01.jpg)
  - Bit to Exabyte, Super ICT 150++ Questions Book (chapter-wise previous questions) — IP Addressing and Subnetting, Q77 — [bit to exabyte/62_bte-g2-p48-ip-q75-79-networking-q01-06.md](../bit%20to%20exabyte/62_bte-g2-p48-ip-q75-79-networking-q01-06.md) · [image](../bit%20to%20exabyte/62_bte-g2-p48-ip-q75-79-networking-q01-06.jpg)
  - duranta.app question bank (coaching compilation of the question paper) — Bangladesh Civil Service – 38th (ICT), Date: 2018 — Time: 3 Hours, Full marks: 200 (pp. 1–18), 7.(a) — [duranta/1706697857-38th-bcs-lecturer-ict/1706697857.md](../duranta/1706697857-38th-bcs-lecturer-ict/1706697857.md)
  - duranta.app question bank (coaching compilation of the question paper) — Bangladesh Civil Service – 38th (ICT), Date: 2018 — Time: 3 Hours, Full marks: 200 (pp. 1–18), 7.(b) — [duranta/1706697857-38th-bcs-lecturer-ict/1706697857.md](../duranta/1706697857-38th-bcs-lecturer-ict/1706697857.md)
  - duranta.app question bank (coaching suggestion, not a dated paper) — suggestion — 46th & 47th BCS Written Suggestions, CSE 971 & ICT 281 (BPSC technical cadre written), Duranta Career & Skills Academy, no time/marks scheme printed, pp. 1-16 — [duranta/1732968747-46th-47th-bcs-written-suggestions-code-971/1732968747.md](../duranta/1732968747-46th-47th-bcs-written-suggestions-code-971/1732968747.md)
- **Printed answer:** 3FFE:85B:1F1F::A9:1234 is IPv6 and has 128 bits. Printed expansion: 3FFE:85B:1F1F:0000:0000:0000:00A9:1234.
- **Correction:** Minor: the printed expansion leaves out the leading zero of the second group. The fully expanded form is 3FFE:085B:1F1F:0000:0000:0000:00A9:1234. The source prints 'IFIF' (letter I), which should be 1F1F.

  - duranta.app question bank (coaching compilation of the question paper) — Dutch Bangla Bank — Assistant Network/Hardware Engineer — 05/03/19 — Exam Taker: BUET — Time 1:30 Hours — Marks distribution: 20 (non_dept) + 65 (dept.) = 85 — pages 1–2, Q6 — [duranta/1707373769-dbbl-2019-written/1707373769.md](../duranta/1707373769-dbbl-2019-written/1707373769.md)

- **Correction:** 255.255.240.0 is a /20 mask, which subnets a **class B** network (default /16): 2^4 = 16 subnets of 2^12 − 2 = 4094 hosts each. On a class C network the third octet is not available for subnetting.

### Explain how DNS works using "mygov.bd" as an example (stem on a missing page). <sub>G0019</sub>

Explain how DNS works using "mygov.bd" as an example (stem on a missing page).

- **Asked in (1):** 45th BCS CSE (2024)

<a id="g0656"></a>

### Difference between POST and GET methods. <sub>G0656</sub>

*Viva*

Difference between POST and GET methods.

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p24 Q35 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p24)
- **Printed answer:** GET: Retrieves data; parameters appear in the URL; POST: Sends data securely in the request body and is used for creating or updating data
- **Correction:** POST is not "secure" by itself: the body is sent in plain text unless HTTPS is used. POST only keeps data out of the URL, browser history and logs; it is also not idempotent and has no practical size limit, unlike GET.

<a id="g0361"></a>

### If asked to set up a network in a room of the OIC office, at which layers would you work … <sub>G0361</sub>

- **Viva mentions:** 1

*Study note*

- **Printed answer:** Symmetric: fast, key sharing hard; asymmetric: slower, solves key distribution, signatures; hash: one-way, integrity.
- **Correction:** MD5 and SHA-1 are listed as hash examples without noting they are broken (collisions) and unfit for security use.

<a id="g0456"></a>

### Compare symmetric and asymmetric cryptography by number of keys, speed, security, key sha… <sub>G0456</sub>

*Study note*

Compare symmetric and asymmetric cryptography by number of keys, speed, security, key sharing and examples.

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Source:**
  - Paper not shown on the source sheet, Section: Symmetric vs asymmetric — [PDF-TEXT/Cryptography sheet.md](../PDF-TEXT/Cryptography%20sheet.md#p2)
- **Printed answer:** Symmetric: one key, faster, key sharing difficult (AES, DES); asymmetric: two keys, slower, key sharing easier (RSA, ECC).
- **Correction:** States symmetric is "less secure" and asymmetric "more secure". Security depends on the algorithm and key length, not on symmetric vs asymmetric: AES-128/256 is at least as strong as RSA-2048/3072. The real trade-off is key distribution (hard for symmetric) vs speed (asymmetric is slower and needs much longer keys).

<a id="g4656"></a>

### Suppose Bangladesh bank is designing a nation of communication network for office located… <sub>G4656</sub>

*Written*

Suppose Bangladesh bank is designing a nation of communication network for office located in Dhaka and Rangpur. Their offices are connected via wide area network (WAN). The team needs to ensure that applications with low latency requirements (such as VOIP and video conferencing) can operate efficiently between these offices. Given: Distance between Dhaka and Rangpur = 30 km; Speed of fiber optic link = 2 × 10^8 m/s; Link capacity = 1 Gbps; Average size of data packets = 15,000 bytes; Delay at each router/switch along the path = 5 ms; RTT between Dhaka and Rangpur = 50 ms. Calculate the total network latency between office at Dhaka and Rangpur, considering the propagation delay and transmission delay.

- **Asked in (1):** BB AD(ICT) 2025
- **Source:**
  - Question paper found on the web (see the folder README for the URL) — Bangladesh Bank — Assistant Director (ICT) (Job ID 271, circular 76/2023) — Written — 07.02.2025 — Exam taker: not stated (coaching labels FBS / DU; unverified) — marks not printed — recollection, R7 — [bank-written-web/2025-bb-adict-written-itjobqns/bb-adict-2025-written.md](../bank-written-web/2025-bb-adict-written-itjobqns/bb-adict-2025-written.md)
- **Correction:** the number of routers/switches is not given; the "2 devices" is the solver's assumption. Propagation + transmission alone = 0.27 ms.

### What is a router? <sub>G0419</sub>

*Viva*

What is a router?

  - NTRCA 17th registration viva 2023 (2023) (viva), Q8 — [VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md) · [image](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.jpg)

- **Printed answer:** A network hardware device that routes data packets between networks toward their destination

<a id="g0418"></a>

### What is a gateway? <sub>G0418</sub>

*Viva*

What is a gateway?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - NTRCA 17th registration viva 2023 (2023) (viva), Q7 — [VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md) · [image](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** A device connecting different networks with protocol translation; can work at all OSI layers but mostly at the upper (transport to application) layers

<a id="g0420"></a>

### Main difference between gateway and router? <sub>G0420</sub>

*Viva*

Main difference between gateway and router?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - NTRCA 17th registration viva 2023 (2023) (viva), Q9 — [VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md) · [image](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Router forwards packets between networks using the same protocol; gateway connects networks with different protocols and translates between them

<a id="g0421"></a>

### What are a hub and a switch? Main difference? <sub>G0421</sub>

*Viva*

What are a hub and a switch? Main difference?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - NTRCA 17th registration viva 2023 (2023) (viva), Q10 — [VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.md) · [image](../VIVA/06_ntrca-topology-url-and-osi.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Hub broadcasts every packet to all ports without checking the destination; switch checks the destination address and forwards only to that device

<a id="g0500"></a>

### What is the main function of a Router? <sub>G0500</sub>

*Viva*

What is the main function of a Router?

<a id="g0501"></a>

### What is a Switch? <sub>G0501</sub>

*Viva*

What is a Switch?

- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p3 Q5 (+p7 Q15) — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p3); [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p7)

Difference between Switch and Router.

<a id="g0539"></a>

### Can a switch communicate among multiple computers? <sub>G0539</sub>

*Viva*

Can a switch communicate among multiple computers?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p9 Q6 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p9)
- **Printed answer:** Yes. A switch connects multiple computers within the same LAN and forwards data only to the intended destination using MAC addresses

<a id="g0543"></a>

### Is a Hub obsolete? Why? <sub>G0543</sub>

*Viva*

Is a Hub obsolete? Why?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p9 Q11 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p9)
- **Printed answer:** Yes. A Hub is mostly obsolete because it sends data to all connected devices, causing collisions and poor performance. Switches are faster and more secure

<a id="g0545"></a>

### Difference between Router, Hub, and Switch. <sub>G0545</sub>

*Viva*

Difference between Router, Hub, and Switch.

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p9 Q13 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p9); [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p10)
- **Printed answer:** Device Function; Hub; Broadcasts data to all connected devices; Switch Sends data only to the intended device using MAC addresses; Router Connects different networks and routes packets using IP addresses

<a id="g0546"></a>

### Can a network be built using only a Router? <sub>G0546</sub>

*Viva*

Can a network be built using only a Router?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p10 Q14 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p10)
- **Printed answer:** No. A router alone cannot efficiently connect multiple wired devices because it has limited LAN ports. A switch is usually needed to expand the network

<a id="g0547"></a>

### Can a network be built using only a Hub? <sub>G0547</sub>

*Viva*

Can a network be built using only a Hub?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p10 Q15 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p10)
- **Printed answer:** Yes, but not recommended. A hub can connect multiple devices, but it broadcasts all traffic, causing collisions and poor network performance

<a id="g0020"></a>

### TCP and UDP are examples of transport layer protocols. What are the differences between t… <sub>G0020</sub>

*Written*

TCP and UDP are examples of transport layer protocols. What are the differences between these two protocols?

- **Asked in (1):** 45th BCS CSE (2024)
- **Source:**
  - 45th BCS Written (CSE-971) (2024-01-31), (b) (question number not shown) — [BCS/07_dns-tcp-udp-differences.md](../BCS/07_dns-tcp-udp-differences.md) · [image](../BCS/07_dns-tcp-udp-differences.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** TCP: connection-oriented, extensive error checking, 20-byte header, heavyweight (HTTP, FTP, SMTP). UDP: connectionless, checksum only, 8-byte header, lightweight (DNS, DHCP, TFTP, SNMP, VoIP).

### Differentiate blacklisting and whitelisting. Which is more secure and why? <sub>G0175</sub>

*Written*

Differentiate blacklisting and whitelisting. Which is more secure and why?

- **Asked in (1):** PGCB AE(CSE) 2021
- **Source:**
  - PGCB Assistant Engineer (CSE) (BUET) (2021-09-30), Q6 — [BUET JOB/13_pattern-processor-widths-blacklist.md](../BUET%20JOB/13_pattern-processor-widths-blacklist.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/13_pattern-processor-widths-blacklist.jpg); [BUET JOB/04_heap-insert-and-delete-7.md](../BUET%20JOB/04_heap-insert-and-delete-7.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/04_heap-insert-and-delete-7.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Defines both; concludes 'Blacklist is more secure'.
- **Correction:** Whitelisting is more secure: it is default-deny (only pre-approved items run), while a blacklist only blocks known-bad items and misses new or unknown threats.

What is Honeypot?

What is NGFW?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1

*Viva*

Difference between NGFW and WAF.

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1

- **Printed answer:** NGFW: Protects the entire network from various threats; WAF: Specifically protects web applications from web-based attacks

What is IDS?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source

What is IPS?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source

What is optical fibre? What principle does it follow?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**

- **Printed answer:** Very thin glass (silica) or plastic fibre carrying light; works by total internal reflection between core and cladding, light travelling in a zig-zag path

How can you detect that a network cable is cut?

- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - BPSC viva (viva), Q4 — [VIVA/02_bpsc-oop-and-dbms-viva.md](../VIVA/02_bpsc-oop-and-dbms-viva.md) · [image](../VIVA/02_bpsc-oop-and-dbms-viva.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Using a cable tester to check whether the cable is good or cut

<a id="g0372"></a>

### How is the light coming out of an optical fibre converted into network bits? <sub>G0372</sub>

*Viva*

How is the light coming out of an optical fibre converted into network bits?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - BPSC viva (viva), Q5 — [VIVA/02_bpsc-oop-and-dbms-viva.md](../VIVA/02_bpsc-oop-and-dbms-viva.md) · [image](../VIVA/02_bpsc-oop-and-dbms-viva.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Says a device called an 'optical modulator' does it; describes light source at transmitter and receiver converting light back to an electronic signal
- **Correction:** Light-to-bits conversion is done by a photodetector (photodiode/PIN/APD) in the optical receiver or transceiver; the modulator/laser or LED does the electrical-to-light conversion at the transmitter.

<a id="g0534"></a>

### What is 10Base-T? <sub>G0534</sub>

What is 10Base-T?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**

- **Printed answer:** 10Base-T is an Ethernet standard that provides 10 Mbps data transmission over twisted-pair (UTP) cables. It commonly uses RJ-45 connectors

- **Printed answer:** The maximum cable length for 10Base-T is 100 meters (328 feet) between a device and a switch or hub

<a id="g0344"></a>

### Total latency for a 3 KB e-mail over a 1 Gbps network, distance 300 km, light speed 3×10^… <sub>G0344</sub>

*Written*

Total latency for a 3 KB e-mail over a 1 Gbps network, distance 300 km, light speed 3×10^8 m/s, RTT 50 ms, queuing time 5 ms.

- **Asked in (1):** BB AD(ICT) 2025
- **Source:**
  - BB AD(ICT) (DU) (2025-02-06), Q2 — [Network/05_total-latency-delay-questions.md](../Network/05_total-latency-delay-questions.md) · [image](../Network/05_total-latency-delay-questions.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Tt = 24000/10^9 = 0.024 ms; propagation step cut off
- **Correction:** Solution incomplete on these pages. Tp = 300000/(3×10^8) = 1 ms, so one-way latency = 1 + 0.024 + 5 = 6.024 ms (the given RTT is not needed for this).

- **Asked in (1):** BB AME 2023
- **Source:**
  - Bangladesh Bank Assistant Maintenance Engineer (BIBM) (2023-02-04), Q4 — [BANK/07_delta-ascii-tv-rate-packetization.md](../BANK/07_delta-ascii-tv-rate-packetization.md) · [image](../BANK/07_delta-ascii-tv-rate-packetization.jpg); [BANK/05_packetization-delay-and-cloud-models.md](../BANK/05_packetization-delay-and-cloud-models.md) · [image](../BANK/05_packetization-delay-and-cloud-models.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Payload 1495 B = 11,960 bits; 11,960 / 128,000 ≈ 0.0935 s
- **Correction:** Minor: 11,960/128,000 = 0.09344 s (≈93.4 ms), printed 0.0935. The textbook (Kurose) version, where all 1500 bytes are voice, gives 93.75 ms.

Consider sending a digitally encoded voice source directly. Suppose the source is encoded a constant rate of 128 kbps. Assume each packet is entirely filled before the source sends the packet into the network. The time required to fill a packet is the packetization delay. Determine the packetization delay for length L = 1500 bytes (roughly corresponding to maximum- sized Ethernet packet).

Determine the transmission time of the packet, $d_{trans}$, in terms of L and R. c. Suppose Host A begins to transmit the packet at time $t = 0$. At time $t = d_{trans}$, where is the last bit of the packet? d. Suppose $d_{prop}$ is greater than $d_{trans}$. At time $t = d_{trans}$, where is the first bit of the packet? e. Suppose, $s = 2.5 \times 10^8$ m/s, and $L = 120$ bits, and $R = 56$ kbps. Find the distance m so that $d_{prop}$ equals $d_{trans}$.

### Explain the parity method for error detection and write the ASCII bit string of "Delta". <sub>G0862</sub>

*Written*

Explain the parity method for error detection and write the ASCII bit string of "Delta".

- **Asked in (1):** BB AME 2023
- **Source:**
  - Bangladesh Bank Assistant Maintenance Engineer (BIBM) (2023-02-04), Q2 — [BANK/01_parity-method-and-delta-ascii.md](../BANK/01_parity-method-and-delta-ascii.md) · [image](../BANK/01_parity-method-and-delta-ascii.jpg); [BANK/07_delta-ascii-tv-rate-packetization.md](../BANK/07_delta-ascii-tv-rate-packetization.md) · [image](../BANK/07_delta-ascii-tv-rate-packetization.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Even-parity example 1011 → 10111; Delta = 01000100 01100101 01101100 01110100 01100001
- **Correction:** Example error: received 10110 has three 1s (odd), so even parity flags an error; the book says two 1s / error-free. Codes listed as '68 65 6C 74 61' and called decimal mix decimal and hex (decimal 68 101 108 116 97; hex 44 65 6C 74 61). The bit strings themselves are correct.

### What is Multicast? <sub>G0554</sub>

*Viva*

What is Multicast?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p11 Q2 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p11)
- **Printed answer:** Multicast is a communication method where data is sent from one sender to a selected group of receivers. It saves bandwidth by sending one copy to multiple intended users

<a id="g0555"></a>

### What is Broadcast? <sub>G0555</sub>

*Viva*

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source

- **Printed answer:** Broadcast is a communication method where data is sent from one device to all devices on the same network segment

- **Printed answer:** Broadcast: Sends data to every device in the network; Multicast: Sends data only to a specific group of subscribed devices

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source

- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p9 Q4 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p9)

<a id="g0538"></a>
